Qatar & Middle East — Daily·Weekly Media Monitor

Twice a day (07:00 and 15:30 Qatar time), major coverage from Qatari, Iranian, other major countries' and Korean outlets is compiled into AI issue summaries with source links. A list of the latest reports from relevant institutions is also available for reference lower on the page, and every Sunday a weekly report recapping the past week is published. [Commercial Section, Embassy of the Republic of Korea in Qatar · KOTRA Doha]

🧭 Key issue briefs AI summary
Issue 1

⚔️ Houthi Expansion Along Yemen's Red Sea Coast and Escalating Saudi/Red Sea Tensions

Key summary
  • The Houthi rebels have seized control of Yemen's Red Sea coastal areas, expanding their sphere of influence and reorganizing a multi-billion dollar commercial sector to run a war economy; Iran denies involvement in the fighting in Yemen, but domestically frames the Houthi advance as a strengthening of the 'Axis of Resistance.'
  • Houthi ballistic missile and drone attacks struck southern Saudi Arabia, injuring at least 13 civilians.
  • The Saudi-led coalition carried out airstrikes on Taiz province in southwestern Yemen, killing at least 3 people, including 2 children.

Issue 2

⛽ Closure of Saudi East-West Pipeline Triggers Surge in Strait of Hormuz Traffic and Global Oil Prices

Key summary
  • Saudi Arabia's East-West Pipeline was shut down due to drone attack damage, blocking the overland route used as an alternative crude transport path, and it has been confirmed that Saudi Arabia is once again increasing crude oil shipments through the Strait of Hormuz.
  • As a result, international oil prices surged to multi-month highs.
  • Traffic through the Strait of Hormuz has been tallied as showing a declining trend despite the recent escalation of attacks in the Middle East.
  • Saudi Arabia is reportedly seeking to further increase tanker transit through the Strait of Hormuz.

Issue 3

🇰🇷 Domestic Concerns Over Oil Supply and Public Opposition to Dispatching Troops to the Strait of Hormuz

Key summary
  • Domestically, concerns have been raised that as securing Middle Eastern crude oil becomes increasingly difficult, support measures for importing non-Middle Eastern crude oil are lagging.
  • Civil society elders held a press conference opposing the government's plan to dispatch troops to the Strait of Hormuz, urging its withdrawal.
  • Some domestic analyses suggest that hardline Iranian factions' attack on Hormuz overturned the peace agreement with the United States.

Issue 5

🇮🇷 Iran Nuclear Talks, IAEA Dispute, and US-Iran Dialogue Tensions

Key summary
  • It has been alleged that the United States pressured the Austrian government to revoke the visa of Iran's Atomic Energy Organization head Mohammad Eslami, preventing him from attending the IAEA meeting in Vienna; Iran has protested this as a violation of member states' rights.
  • Talks between Iran and Gulf states scheduled to be held in Oman were postponed, dealing a blow to expectations for a breakthrough related to the Iran war.

Issue 6

💵 Aftermath of the US-Iran War: Tally of US Military Losses and Sanctions on Iran

Key summary
  • The Pentagon watchdog released its first official tally of losses related to the Iran war, stating that hundreds of buildings and aircraft were damaged, approximately $33.4 billion was spent, and a strategic-level shortage of ammunition stockpiles resulted.
  • Assessments indicate that bottlenecks occurred in the US Department of Defense's supply chain, and the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation's (SCO) counter-terrorism body condemned the US airstrikes on Iran.
  • The US Department of Justice stated that millions of dollars from Iranian oil sales were laundered through Binance.

This page automatically collects and summarizes public news coverage and material from relevant institutions across Qatar, Iran, other major countries and Korea. News is gathered by querying Google News RSS feeds, Naver News search, and outlet RSS (Al Jazeera, BBC, etc.) with keywords such as Qatar, the Middle East and energy; in-depth reports are collected directly from the publication boards and RSS of domestic and international research institutes, international organizations and consultancies (supplemented by news search targeting those publishers), keeping only analyses and reports on the Middle East situation, the Iran war and their industrial, supply-chain and energy repercussions. The report list accumulates items published since the war's outbreak (2026-02-28), split into domestic and overseas and ordered by publication date (newest first). Collection and summarization rely on each outlet's publicly available headline and snippet; for subscription or paywalled outlets, the full article can be opened through the provided source link only after the reader signs in or subscribes to that outlet directly. The collected articles are automatically clustered and summarized by issue, and translated into English and Arabic, by Anthropic Claude (Sonnet 5) AI. The whole process runs and publishes automatically twice a day (07:00 and 15:30 Qatar time) via GitHub Actions. The Qatar government activity section separately curates only official statements, announcements and actions on the war and regional situation by Qatar government and state entities — the Emir, the Prime Minister, the ministries of Foreign Affairs, Defense and Interior, the Government Communications Office (GCO), QatarEnergy and the state news agency (QNA). Owing to automated collection some articles or reports may be missed, so please re-verify important issues against the original sources and further searches.
- Embassy of the Republic of Korea in Qatar, Commercial Section · KOTRA Doha
